) for all
\eta
-Bethe states under consideration.
(Due to the range
m_{s}\in[0,(1-m_{\eta})]
, only the
m_{s}=0
S
z
SLN
SN
subspace 1A
exists in the
m_{\eta}\rightarrow 1
limit.)

The number of electronic charges carried by each charge carrier provides
a complementary physically appealing reason of why for
u>0
and any
S
z
S subspace all charge pseudoparticles of the
\eta
-Bethe
state whose charge current has largest absolute value are
c
pseudoparticles.
Indeed, the charges carried by the electrons remain invariant under the electron - rotated-electron
unitary transformation. Hence each rotated electrons carries one electronic charge.
Within the charge - spin degrees of freedom separation of the rotated-electron lattice
occupancy configurations that generate the
\eta
-Bethe states, each
c
pseudoparticle carries a single electronic
charge. Moreover, each
\eta n
pseudoparticle carries a number
2n
of electronic charges.
The number,
L-2S_{\eta}
, of electronic charges of the
\eta
-Bethe states that span a
S
z
S subspace is the same for all of them.
For general
\eta
-Bethe states, a number
N_{c}
of such electronic charges are carried by

N_{c}

c
pseudoparticles and the remaining
\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}2n\,N_{\eta n}

electronic charges are carried by
N_{\eta}=\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}N_{\eta n}

\eta n

pseudoparticles.

On the one hand, the exact sum rule,
N_{c}+\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}2n\,N_{\eta n}=L-2S_{\eta}
,
thus follows from charge conservation. On the other hand, the values of the number of
carriers
N_{\rho}=N_{c}+N_{\eta}
of a
\eta
-Bethe state vary in the interval
N_{\rho}\in[1,(L-2S_{\eta})]

for the corresponding set of S
z
SLN subspaces contained in a S
z
S subspace
for which
2S_{\eta}
is even. Each
\eta n
pseudoparticle of such states carries
2n
electronic charges and
accordingly is a
heavier
object than a
c
pseudoparticle. It is thus a physically expected and appealing result that
a
\eta
-Bethe state whose
L-2S_{\eta}
electronic charges are carried by
L-2S_{\eta}
independent charge carriers,
each carrying a single electronic charge, is that whose charge current absolute value
is the largest of the corresponding S
z
S subspace. This argument is consistent with

N_{\rho}=N_{c}=L-2S_{\eta}
for the reference S
z
SLN subspace 1 of a S
z
S subspace.

Appendix D
Effects on the charge currents of the deviations from the TBA ideal strings

For a large finite system, the
n>1
complex rapidities with the same real part
deviate from their TL ideal form, Eq. (
28
). This affects both
the
\alpha=s
spin and
\alpha=\eta

\eta
-spin complex rapidities.
In some solvable models small effects of such deviations may survive even in the TL.
In the present case of the charge currents of the 1D Hubbard model, only the deviations of the

n>1
charge
\eta
complex rapidities, Eq. (
28
) for
\alpha=\eta
,
may have effects on the absolute values of the charge currents of some
classes of
\eta
-Bethe states. (For an interesting study on the small effects of the
\alpha=\eta
deviations
on the charge degrees of freedom of the 1D Hubbard model, see Ref.
Deguchi-00
.)

The set of
l=1,...,n
distorted charge
\alpha=\eta
complex rapidities with the same real part under consideration have the general form

\Lambda^{\eta n,l}(q_{j})=\Lambda^{\eta n}(q_{j})+i\,(n+1-2l)\,u+D_{j}^{\eta n,l}
.
Here
D_{j}^{\eta n,l}=R_{j}^{\eta n,l}+i\delta_{j}^{\eta n,l}
, where
R_{j}^{\eta n,l}
and
\delta_{j}^{\eta n,l}

are real numbers, is the fine-structure deviation from the TBA ideal charge
\eta n
strings.
Importantly,
D_{j}^{\eta 1,1}=0
for the
N_{\eta 1}
rapidities
\Lambda^{\eta 1,1}(q_{j})

of all energy and momentum eigenstates. Indeed, both the
c
momentum rapidities and such
\eta 1
rapidities are real
and thus lack such deviations.

The
n>1
distorted complex rapidities

\Lambda^{\eta n,l}(q_{j})=\Lambda^{\eta n}(q_{j})+i\,(n+1-2l)\,u+D_{j}^{\eta n,l}

remain being labelled by the quantum numbers
n=2,...,\infty
and
l=1,...,n
that refer to the number of
\eta
-spin
singlet pairs and each of these pairs, respectively. Physically, this means that, as in the case of
an ideal charge
\eta n
string, for
n>1
the distorted charge
\eta n
string associated with that set of complex rapidities
also describes an independent configuration within which

n=2,...,\infty

\eta
-spin singlet pairs are bound.

The set of
l=1,...,n
TBA ideal charge
\eta n
complex rapidities with the same real part, Eq. (
28
) for
\alpha=\eta
,
obey the symmetry relation
\Lambda^{\eta n,l}(q_{j})=(\Lambda^{\eta n,n+1-l}(q_{j}))^{*}
.
The two complex rapidities
\Lambda^{\eta n,l}(q_{j})
and
\Lambda^{\eta n,l^{\prime}}(q_{j})
associated with
two
\eta
-spin singlet pairs labelled by the quantum numbers
l
and
l^{\prime}=n+1-l
, respectively, are related as

\Lambda^{\eta n,l}(q_{j})=(\Lambda^{\eta n,l^{\prime}}(q_{j}))^{*}
for
l=1,...,n
. This is actually a necessary condition for the binding of the

l=1,...,n

\eta
-spin singlet pairs within the
\eta n
-pair configuration.

Importantly and due to self-conjugacy, the deviations
D_{j}^{\eta n,l}=R_{j}^{\eta n,l}+i\delta_{j}^{\eta n,l}

for the set of
l=1,...,n
complex rapidities with the same real part associated with a
n>1
distorted
charge
\eta n
string are also such that
D_{j}^{\eta n,l}=(D_{j}^{\eta n,n+1-l})^{*}
. That
the symmetry
\Lambda^{\eta n,l}(q_{j})=(\Lambda^{\eta n,n+1-l}(q_{j}))^{*}
prevails under string deformations
ensures that, as for the ideal
\eta n
strings, the imaginary part of the
n>1
real rapidities with the
same real part associated with deformed charge
\eta n
strings also describe the
binding within the corresponding
n
-pair configurations of
l=1,...,n

\eta
-singlet pairs.

As for the
n>1
complex spin rapidities of the related spin-
1/2

XXX
chain
CPC
;
CP
,
the
collapse of narrow pairs
, which within our representation refers to

\eta
-singlet pair unbinding processes, is in the TL the only aberration from the
n>1
ideal
charge
\eta n
strings that may have effects on the charge currents. The occurrence for the
1D Hubbard model of two types
c
and
\eta 1
of real charge rapidities that are insensitive to such effects
renders them even less important than for the spin-
1/2

XXX

chain
CPC
;
CP
. Indeed, for that chain there is only one branch of real rapidities.
As in the case of its spin currents, in the TL the small effects under
consideration have no impact whatsoever in the
T>0
stiffness upper
bounds considered in Sections
V
and
VI
.

The very small effects of charge
\eta n
string deviations occur through the
n>1
current spectra

J_{\eta n}(q_{j})=-J_{\eta n}^{h}(q_{j})
, Eq. (
121
) of Appendix
A
and
Eq. (
41
), in the charge currents general expression, Eq. (
120
) of that Appendix and Eq. (
40
).
This applies only to
\eta
-Bethe states described by groups of real charge
c
and
\eta 1
rapidities and
n>1
complex charge
\eta n
rapidities.
This follows from the dependence of such current
spectra on the complex charge
\eta n
rapidities
\Lambda^{\eta n,l}(q_{j})=\Lambda^{\eta n}(q_{j})+i\,(n+1-2l)\,u+D_{j}^{\eta n,l}
associated with

n>1
bound
\eta
-spin singlet pairs.

The charge
\eta n
string deviations from the TBA
n>1
ideal charge
\eta n
strings do not change the number
of
\eta
-spin singlet pairs. Their density remains being exactly
given by
\pi_{\eta}=(l_{\eta}-m_{\eta})/2
for the corresponding
\eta
-Bethe states and non-LWSs.
Narrow pairs refer to a string deformation originated by a deviation
D_{j}^{\eta n,l}

that renders the separation between two rapidities

\Lambda^{\eta n,l}(q_{j})
and
\Lambda^{\eta n,l+1}(q_{j})
in the imaginary direction less than
i\,u
.
Such a separation may become narrower and eventually merge and split back onto
the horizontal axis. (This is why such a process is called the collapse of a narrow pair.)

Within our representation in terms of paired rotated
\eta
-spins
1/2
, each collapse of a narrow
pair leads to the unbinding of two
\eta
-spin singlet pairs. On the one hand, for the
set of
n>2
complex charge
\eta n
rapidities with the same real part associated with
n
bound

\eta
-spin singlet pairs, it leads to the partition of the
corresponding
\eta n
-pair configuration into a
\eta n^{\prime}
-pair configuration where
n^{\prime}=n-2

and two unbound
\eta
-spin singlet pairs described by real charge
\eta 1
rapidities.
The
\eta n^{\prime}
-pair configuration is described by a smaller number
n^{\prime}=n-2
of complex charge
\eta n
rapidities with the same
real part in a charge
\eta n^{\prime}
string of smaller length
n^{\prime}=n-2
. On the
other hand, for complex charge
\eta 2
rapidities with the same real part it leads in turn
to the unbinding of the two
\eta
-spin singlet pairs of the corresponding
\eta 2
-pair configuration.
In this case this gives rise solely to the two unbound
\eta
-spin singlet pairs described by real charge
\eta 1
rapidities.

Hence the collapse of a narrow pair is a process that causes an increase in the value of
the total number of
\eta n
pseudoparticles
N_{\eta}
, Eq. (
30
) for
\alpha=\eta
,
and thus in the equal number
N_{\eta}
of charge
\eta n
strings of all lengths
n=1,...,\infty
. It does not change though that of

\eta
-spin singlet pairs,
\Pi_{\eta}=\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}n\,N_{\eta n}=(L_{\eta}-2S_{\eta})/2
, Eq. (
18
)
for
\alpha=\eta
. The number of rotated
\eta
-spins
L_{\eta}=2S_{\eta}+2\Pi_{\eta}

and the corresponding density
l_{\eta}>m_{\eta}
thus remain unchanged under such charge
\eta n
string distortions.

The upper bounds used in our procedures within the canonical and grand-canonical ensembles
rely on the largest current absolute value of
\eta
-Bethe states in each S
z
S subspace
and on averages of current absolute values of
\eta
-Bethe states described by only real
rapidities, respectively. In both cases, the
\eta
-Bethe states carrying the charge currents
under consideration have density
l_{\eta}\rightarrow m_{\eta}
.
Hence they are insensitive to the collapse of narrow pairs.
Indeed, the corresponding increase in the number
N_{\eta}
of charge
\eta n
strings of all lengths does not
generate states with current absolute values larger than those of such
\eta
-Bethe states.
This is why the collapse of narrow pairs has no affects
whatsoever in the upper bounds used in the studies of this paper.

Appendix E
The 1D Hubbard model global symmetry group independent state representations and Hilbert space and subspaces dimensions

Following the 1D Hubbard model global
[SU(2)\otimes SU(2)\otimes U(1)]/Z_{2}^{2}
symmetry
bipartite
, its
full Hilbert-space dimension
4^{L}
must equal the number of independent state representations of the
corresponding symmetry group. In each subspace with fixed values for
L_{s}=N_{c}
,
L_{\eta}=N_{c}^{h}
,
S_{s}
, and
S_{\eta}
there are

{\cal{N}}^{\,\eta}(L_{\eta},S_{\eta})\times{\cal{N}}^{s}(L_{s},S_{s})\times d_{c}(N_{c})
such representations. Here,

{\cal{N}}^{\,\alpha}(L_{\alpha},S_{\alpha})=(2S_{\alpha}+1)\,{\cal{N}}_{\rm singlet}^{\,\alpha}(L_{\alpha},S_{\alpha})\hskip 5.69046pt{\rm and}\hskip 5.69046ptd_{c}={L\choose L_{s}}={L\choose L_{\eta}}\hskip 5.69046pt{\rm for}\hskip 5.69046pt\alpha=\eta,s\,,

(145)

where,

{\cal{N}}_{\rm singlet}^{\,\alpha}(L_{\alpha},S_{\alpha})={L_{\alpha}\choose L_{\alpha}/2-S_{\alpha}}-{L_{\alpha}\choose L_{\alpha}/2-S_{\alpha}-1}\hskip 5.69046pt{\rm for}\hskip 5.69046pt\alpha=\eta,s\,.

(146)

The
\alpha=\eta,s

SU(2)
dimensions are similar to the spin
SU(2)
dimension of the spin-
1/2

XXX
chain
CPC
;
CP
.
The dimension
d_{c}={L\choose N_{c}}
is characteristic of an
U(1)
symmetry and refers indeed to the
c
lattice
U(1)
symmetry.

One finds that,

\displaystyle 4^{L}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}N_{c}=0\\
({\rm integers})\end{subarray}}^{L}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}2S_{\eta}=0\\
({\rm integers})\end{subarray}}^{L_{\eta}}\,\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}2S_{s}=0\\
({\rm integers})\end{subarray}}^{L_{s}}C(L_{\eta},L_{s},S_{\eta},S_{s})\,{\cal{N}}^{\,\eta}(L_{\eta},S_{\eta})\times{\cal{N}}^{s}(L_{s},S_{s})\times d_{c}(N_{c})\,,

(147)

\displaystyle C(L_{\eta},L_{s},S_{\eta},S_{s})=|\cos\left({\pi\over 2}(2S_{\eta}+L_{\eta})\right)\cos\left({\pi\over 2}(2S_{s}+L_{s})\right)|\,,

where the role of the phase factor,
C(N_{c},S_{\eta},S_{s})=0,1
, is to select the allowed independent representations of the global
[SU(2)\otimes SU(2)\otimes U(1)]/Z_{2}^{2}
symmetry.

As for the corresponding spin-singlet dimension of the
XXX
chain
CPC
;
CP
, the dimension
{\cal{N}}_{\rm singlet}(S_{\alpha},M_{\alpha})

in Eq. (
145
) can be written as,

{\cal{N}}_{\rm singlet}^{\alpha}(L_{\alpha},S_{\alpha})=\sum_{\{N_{\alpha n}\}}\,\prod_{n=1}^{\infty}\,{L_{\alpha n}\choose N_{\alpha n}}\hskip 5.69046pt{\rm for}\hskip 5.69046pt\alpha=\eta,s\,,

(148)

where
\sum_{\{N_{\alpha n}\}}
is a summation over all sets of
\{N_{\alpha n}\}
corresponding to the same number of
pairs,
\Pi_{\alpha}=\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}n\,N_{\alpha n}=(L_{\alpha}-2S_{\alpha})/2
, Eq. (
18
).
(That the use of the alternative dimension expression, Eq. (
148
), in Eq. (
147
) leads to the same overall dimension
4^{L}

is shown in Ref.
Completeness
.)

For the problem studied in this paper, we consider the
S_{s}^{z}=0
subspace. Its
S
z
SL subspaces as defined in Section
III.2
are populated by fixed numbers

L_{\eta}=N_{c}^{h}=L-N_{c}
rotated
\eta
-spins
1/2
of which
2\Pi_{\eta}=L_{\eta}-2S_{\eta}
are paired and the
remaining
M_{\eta}=2S_{\eta}
physical
\eta
-spins
1/2
are unpaired. They are as well
populated by fixed numbers
L_{s}=L-L_{\eta}
of rotated spins
1/2
of which
2\Pi_{s}=L-L_{\eta}-2S_{s}
are paired and the
remaining
M_{s}=2S_{s}
physical spins
1/2
are unpaired.
Since for the different spin
S_{s}
integer values only states for which
S_{s}^{z}=0
contribute,
out of the
2S_{s}+1
states of each spin multiplet tower only that for which
S_{s}^{z}=0

is counted. The dimension of a
S_{s}^{z}=0
subspace thus reads,

d_{\rm subspace}(L_{\eta},S_{\eta})=\sum_{S_{s}=0}^{(L-L_{\eta})/2}(2S_{\eta}+1)\,{\cal{N}}_{\rm singlet}^{\,\eta}(L_{\eta},S_{\eta})\times{\cal{N}}_{\rm singlet}^{s}(L_{\eta},S_{s})\times d_{c}(L_{\eta})\,,

(149)

where accounting for that
L_{s}=L-L_{\eta}
,

\displaystyle{\cal{N}}_{\rm singlet}^{\,\eta}(L_{\eta},S_{\eta})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle{L_{\eta}\choose L_{\eta}/2-S_{\eta}}-{L_{\eta}\choose L_{\eta}/2-S_{\eta}-1}=\sum_{\{N_{\eta n}\}}\,\prod_{n=1}^{\infty}\,{L_{\eta n}\choose N_{\eta n}}\,,

\displaystyle{\cal{N}}_{\rm singlet}^{s}(L_{\eta},S_{s})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle{L-L_{\eta}\choose L/2-L_{\eta}/2-S_{s}}-{L-L_{\eta}\choose L/2-L_{\eta}/2-S_{s}-1}=\sum_{\{N_{sn}\}}\,\prod_{n=1}^{\infty}\,{L_{sn}\choose N_{sn}}\,,

\displaystyle d_{c}(L_{\eta})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle{L\choose L_{\eta}}\,.

(150)

The summations
\sum_{\{N_{\eta n}\}}
and
\sum_{\{N_{sn}\}}
run again over all sets of

\{N_{\eta n}\}
and
\{N_{sn}\}
, respectively, corresponding to the same number of
singlet pairs. That number is given by
\Pi_{\eta}=\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}n\,N_{\eta n}=(L_{\eta}-2S_{\eta})/2

for the
\eta
-spin singlet pairs. For the
S_{s}^{z}=0
subspace the number of spin-singlet pairs
reads
\Pi_{s}=\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}n\,N_{sn}=(L-L_{\eta}-2S_{s})/2
.

An important subspace of a S
z
SL subspace
is that spanned by the corresponding
\eta
-Bethe states whose dimension is given by,

d_{\rm subspace}^{LWS}(L_{\eta},S_{\eta})=\sum_{S_{s}=0}^{(L-L_{\eta})/2}{\cal{N}}_{\rm singlet}^{\,\eta}(L_{\eta},S_{\eta})\times{\cal{N}}_{\rm singlet}^{s}(L_{\eta},S_{s})\times d_{c}(L_{\eta})\,.

(151)

Appendix F
Quantities in the expression of the pseudoparticle elementary currents

The
f
functions in the elementary currents expression, Eq. (
56
), read,

\displaystyle f_{\beta\,\beta^{\prime}}(q_{j},q_{j^{\prime}})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle v_{\beta}(q_{j})\,2\pi\,\Phi_{\beta,\beta^{\prime}}(q_{j},q_{j^{\prime}})+v_{\beta^{\prime}}(q_{j^{\prime}})\,2\pi\,\Phi_{\beta^{\prime},\beta}(q_{j^{\prime}},q_{j})

(152)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle{1\over 2\pi}\sum_{\iota=\pm 1}\theta(N_{c})\,|v_{c}(q_{Fc,\tau}^{\iota})|\,2\pi\,\Phi_{c,\beta}(q_{Fc,\tau}^{\iota},q_{j})\,2\pi\,\Phi_{c,\beta^{\prime}}(q_{Fc,\tau}^{\iota},q_{j^{\prime}})

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle{1\over 2\pi}\sum_{\iota=\pm 1}\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}\theta(N_{\eta n})\,|v_{\eta n}(q_{F\eta n,\tau_{n}}^{\iota})|\,2\pi\,\Phi_{\eta n,\beta}(q_{F\eta n,\tau_{n}}^{\iota},q_{j})\,2\pi\,\Phi_{\eta n,\beta^{\prime}}(q_{F\eta n,\tau_{n}}^{\iota},q_{j^{\prime}})

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle{1\over 2\pi}\sum_{\iota=\pm 1}\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}\theta(N_{sn})\,|v_{sn}(\iota q_{Fsn})|\,2\pi\,\Phi_{sn,\beta}(\iota q_{Fsn},q_{j})\,2\pi\,\Phi_{sn,\beta^{\prime}}(\iota q_{Fsn},q_{j^{\prime}})\,,

where the
\beta=c,\eta n,sn
bands group velocities
v_{\beta}(q_{j})
are within the TL continuum

q
representation given by,

v_{\beta}(q)={\partial\varepsilon_{\beta}(q)\over\partial q}\hskip 5.69046pt{\rm where}\hskip 5.69046pt\beta=c,\eta n,sn\,.

(153)

The
\beta=c,\eta n,sn
band energy dispersions
\varepsilon_{\beta}(q_{j})
appearing here read,

\displaystyle\varepsilon_{\beta}(q_{j})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle E_{\beta}(q_{j})+\varepsilon_{\beta}^{c}(q_{j})\,;\hskip 14.22636pt\varepsilon_{\beta}^{c}(q_{j})={t\over\pi}\int_{\{Q^{\iota}_{\tau}\}}dk\,2\pi\,\bar{\Phi}_{c\,\beta}\left({\sin k\over u},{\Lambda_{0}^{\beta}(q_{j})\over u}\right)\sin k

(154)

\displaystyle{\rm for}\hskip 5.69046ptj=1,...,L_{\beta}\,,

where the
\beta=c,\eta n,sn
bands energy spectrum
E_{\beta}(q_{j})
is provided in
Eq. (
117
) of Appendix
A
with the rapidity functionals

\Lambda_{0}^{c}(q_{j})=\sin k_{0}^{c}(q_{j})
and
\Lambda_{0}^{\alpha n}(q_{j})

being those of a
\eta
-Bethe state with compact distributions of general form, Eq. (
51
).
The integration
\int_{\{Q^{\iota}_{\tau}\}}dk
is defined as,

\displaystyle\int_{\{Q^{\iota}_{\tau}\}}dk

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{-\pi}^{Q^{-}_{-}}dk+\int_{Q^{+}_{-}}^{\pi}dk\hskip 5.69046pt{\rm for}\hskip 5.69046ptm_{\eta}\leq 1/2\hskip 5.69046pt(\tau=-\hskip 4.26773pt{\rm hole}\hskip 5.69046pt{\rm like})

(155)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{Q^{-}_{+}}^{Q^{+}_{+}}dk\hskip 5.69046pt{\rm for}\hskip 5.69046ptm_{\eta}\geq 1/2\hskip 5.69046pt(\tau=+\hskip 4.26773pt{\rm particle}\hskip 5.69046pt{\rm like})\,.

The momentum rapidity variable
k
integration limiting parameters
Q^{\iota}_{\tau}
are given by,

Q^{\iota}_{\tau}=k^{c}(q_{Fc,\tau}^{\iota})\hskip 5.69046pt{\rm where}\hskip 5.69046pt\iota=\pm\hskip 5.69046pt{\rm and}\hskip 5.69046pt\tau=\pm\,.

(156)

In this expression
q_{Fc,\tau}^{\iota}
stands for the
c
band compact domains limiting momenta, Eq. (
52
)
for
\beta=c
.

In the case of excited states of a ground state whose
c
band distribution is compact and symmetrical as
given in Eq. (
59
), the corresponding limiting occupancy momentum rapidities
Q^{\iota}_{\tau}
in
Eq. (
156
) rather read
Q^{\pm}_{+}=\pm Q
for such a ground state where
Q=k^{c}(q_{Fc})

and
q_{Fc}=\pi(1-m_{\eta})
.

For
\eta
-Bethe states generated by a finite number of
c
band processes
from those with
c
and
s1
bands compact distributions of general form, Eq. (
51
), belonging
to the
m_{s}=0
reference S
z
SLN
SN
subspace 1A, the related group velocity, Eq. (
153
) for
\beta=c
,
is up to
{\cal{O}}(u^{-2})
order and for
m_{\eta}\in[0,1]
found to be given by,

\displaystyle v_{c}(q_{j})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2t\sin q_{j}-2t\,{(1-m_{\eta})\ln 2\over u}\sin 2q_{j}-\tau\,2t\,{\ln 2\over 2\pi u}\left(\sum_{\iota=\pm}(\iota)\cos(q_{Fc,\tau}^{\iota})\right)\cos q

(157)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle 6t\,(1-m_{\eta})^{2}\left({\ln 2\over u}\right)^{2}\left(1-{3\over 2}\sin^{2}q_{j}\right)\sin q_{j}-\tau\,2t\,{(1-m_{\eta})\over 2\pi}\left({\ln 2\over u}\right)^{2}\left(\sum_{\iota=\pm}(\iota)\cos(q_{Fc,\tau}^{\iota})\right)\sin q

\displaystyle-

\displaystyle\tau\,2t\,{(1-m_{\eta})\over 2\pi}\left({\ln 2\over u}\right)^{2}\left(\sum_{\iota=\pm}(\iota)\sin^{2}(q_{Fc,\tau}^{\iota})\right)\cos q-2t\,{1\over 8\pi^{2}}\left({\ln 2\over u}\right)^{2}\left(\sum_{\iota=\pm}\sin(2q_{Fc,\tau}^{\iota})\right)\cos q

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle 2t\,{1\over 4\pi^{2}}\left({\ln 2\over u}\right)^{2}\sin(q_{Fc,\tau}^{-}+q_{Fc,\tau}^{+})\,\cos q\,.

The terms that depend on the momenta
q_{Fc,\tau}^{\iota}
where
\tau=\pm
and
\iota=\pm
are
state dependent. The expression corresponding to the subset of terms that are state independent gives exactly
the
c
band current spectrum in Eq. (
72
).

For the
m_{s}\rightarrow 1-m_{\eta}
reference S
z
SLN
SN
subspace 1A
and
m_{\eta}\in[0,1]
all terms of the
c
-band group velocity under consideration are up to
{\cal{O}}(u^{-2})
order state
independent and read,

v_{c}(q_{j})=2t\sin q_{j}-2t\,{(1-m_{\eta}-m_{s})\over u}\sin 2q_{j}+6t\,{(1-m_{\eta}-m_{s})^{2}\over u^{2}}\left(1-{3\over 2}\sin^{2}q_{j}\right)\sin q_{j}\,.

(158)

Note that up to that order the group velocity
v_{c}(q_{j})
exactly equals the current spectrum

J_{c}^{h}(q_{j})=-J_{c}(q_{j})
, Eq. (
73
).

Finally, for
m_{\eta}=0
and the set of fixed
m_{s}
density reference S
z
SLN
SN
subspaces 1A
referring to the interval
m_{s}\in[0,1]
all terms of the
c
-band group velocity
v_{c}(q_{j})

are up to
{\cal{O}}(u^{-2})
order state independent and that velocity
exactly equals the current spectrum
J_{c}^{h}(q_{j})=-J_{c}(q_{j})
, Eq. (
70
),
and thus reads,

\displaystyle v_{c}(q_{j})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2t\sin q_{j}-2t\,{n_{\eta s}\over u}\sin 2q_{j}+6t\,\left({n_{\eta s}\over u}\right)^{2}\left(1-{3\over 2}\sin^{2}q_{j}\right)\sin q_{j}

(159)

\displaystyle{\rm where}\hskip 5.69046ptn_{\eta s}=(1-m_{s})\,g_{s}\hskip 5.69046pt{\rm for}\hskip 5.69046ptm_{\eta}=0\hskip 5.69046pt{\rm and}\hskip 5.69046ptm_{s}\in[0,1]\,.

Here
g_{s}=g_{s}(m_{s})
is the function in Eq. (
71
).

The phase shifts
2\pi\,\Phi_{\beta,\beta^{\prime}}(q_{j},q_{j^{\prime}})
in Eq. (
152
) where
\beta
and

\beta^{\prime}
refers to
c
,
\eta n
, and
sn
branches are a generalization of those considered in Ref.
Carmelo-17
to

\eta
-Bethe states with compact distributions of general form, Eq. (
51
), and
\eta
-Bethe
states generated from them by small
c
and
\eta n
bands distributions deviations.
For all such states they have the form,

2\pi\,\Phi_{\beta,\beta^{\prime}}(q_{j},q_{j^{\prime}})=2\pi\,\bar{\Phi}_{\beta,\beta^{\prime}}\left(r,r^{\prime}\right)\hskip 5.69046pt{\rm where}\hskip 5.69046ptr=\Lambda_{0}^{\beta}(q_{j})/u\hskip 5.69046pt{\rm and}\hskip 5.69046ptr^{\prime}=\Lambda_{0}^{\beta^{\prime}}(q_{j^{\prime}})/u\,,

(160)

and the rapidity functionals are those of the corresponding
\eta
-Bethe states with compact distributions.
The rapidity dressed phase shifts
2\pi\,\bar{\Phi}_{\beta,\beta^{\prime}}
on the right-hand side of Eq. (
160
)
are the solution of well-defined integral equations. Those are provided in the following for the
reference S
z
SLN
SN
subspace 1, as defined in Section
IV.2
.
(That subspace plays a central role in the upper bound
procedures used in the studies of this paper.)

For the case of
\eta
-Bethe states with compact distributions of general
form, Eq. (
51
), belonging to the reference S
z
SLN
SN
subspace 1 and
\eta
-Bethe states generated
from them by small
c
and
\eta n
bands distributions deviations, a first set of rapidity dressed
phase shifts
2\pi\,\bar{\Phi}_{\beta,\beta^{\prime}}
obey integral equations by their own,

\bar{\Phi}_{s1,c}\left(r,r^{\prime}\right)=-{1\over\pi}\arctan(r-r^{\prime})+\int_{-B/u}^{B/u}dr^{\prime\prime}\,G_{\tau}(r,r^{\prime\prime})\,{\bar{\Phi}}_{s1,c}\left(r^{\prime\prime},r^{\prime}\right)\,,

(161)

\bar{\Phi}_{s1,\eta n}\left(r,r^{\prime}\right)=-{1\over{\pi^{2}}\,u}\int_{\{Q^{\iota}_{\tau}\}}dk\,\cos k\,{\arctan\Bigl({\sin k/u-r^{\prime}\over n}\Bigr)\over{1+(r-\sin k/u)^{2}}}+\int_{-B/u}^{B/u}dr^{\prime\prime}\,G_{\tau}(r,r^{\prime\prime})\,{\bar{\Phi}}_{s1,\eta n}\left(r^{\prime\prime},r^{\prime}\right)\,,

(162)

and

\displaystyle\bar{\Phi}_{s1,sn}\left(r,r^{\prime}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\delta_{1,n}\,{1\over\pi}\arctan\Bigl({r-r^{\prime}\over 2}\Bigl)+(1-\delta_{1,n}){1\over\pi}\Bigl\{\arctan\Bigl({r-r^{\prime}\over n-1}\Bigl)+\arctan\Bigl({r-r^{\prime}\over n+1}\Bigl)\Bigr\}

(163)

\displaystyle-

\displaystyle{1\over{\pi^{2}}\,u}\int_{\{Q^{\iota}_{\tau}\}}dk\,\cos k\,{\arctan\Bigl({\sin k/u-r^{\prime}\over n}\Bigr)\over{1+(r-\sin k/u)^{2}}}+\int_{-B/u}^{B/u}dr^{\prime\prime}\,G_{\tau}(r,r^{\prime\prime})\,{\bar{\Phi}}_{s1,s1}\left(r^{\prime\prime},r^{\prime}\right)\,.

The kernel
G_{\tau}(r,r^{\prime})
is given by,

G_{\tau}(r,r^{\prime})=-{1\over{2\pi}}\left[{1\over{1+((r-r^{\prime})/2)^{2}}}\right]\left[1+{\tau\over 2}\left(t_{\tau}(r)+t_{\tau}(r^{\prime})+{{l_{\tau}(r)-l_{\tau}(r^{\prime})}\over{r-r^{\prime}}}\right)\right]\,.

(164)

Here

t_{\tau}(r)={1\over{\pi}}\sum_{\iota=\pm}(\iota)\arctan\left(r-{\sin Q_{\tau}^{\iota}\over u}\right)\,,

(165)

and

l_{\tau}(r)={1\over{\pi}}\sum_{\iota=\pm}(\iota)\ln\left(1+\left(r-{\sin Q_{\tau}^{\iota}\over u}\right)^{2}\right)\,.

(166)

A second set of rapidity dressed phase shifts are expressed in terms of those in Eqs.
(
161
)-(
163
) as follows,

\bar{\Phi}_{c,c}\left(r,r^{\prime}\right)={1\over{\pi}}\int_{-B/u}^{B/u}dr^{\prime\prime}{\bar{\Phi}_{s1,c}\left(r^{\prime\prime},r^{\prime}\right)\over{1+(r-r^{\prime\prime})^{2}}}\,,

(167)

\bar{\Phi}_{c,\eta n}\left(r,r^{\prime}\right)=-{1\over\pi}\arctan\Bigl({r-r^{\prime}\over n}\Bigr)+{1\over{\pi}}\int_{-B/u}^{B/u}dr^{\prime\prime}{\bar{\Phi}_{s1,\eta n}\left(r^{\prime\prime},r^{\prime}\right)\over{1+(r-r^{\prime\prime})^{2}}}\,,

(168)

and

\bar{\Phi}_{c,sn}\left(r,r^{\prime}\right)=-{1\over\pi}\arctan\Bigl({r-r^{\prime}\over n}\Bigr)+{1\over{\pi}}\int_{-B/u}^{B/u}dr^{\prime\prime}{\bar{\Phi}_{s1,sn}\left(r^{\prime\prime},r^{\prime}\right)\over{1+(r-r^{\prime\prime})^{2}}}\,.

(169)

The remaining rapidity dressed phase shifts can be
expressed either in terms of those in Eqs. (
167
)-(
169
) only,

{\bar{\Phi}}_{\eta n,c}\left(r,r^{\prime}\right)={1\over\pi}\arctan\Bigl({r-r^{\prime}\over{n}}\Bigr)-{1\over{\pi}\,u}\int_{\{Q^{\iota}_{\tau}\}}dk\,\cos k\,{{\bar{\Phi}}_{c,c}\left(\sin k/u,r^{\prime}\right)\over{n[1+({r-\sin k/u\over{n}})^{2}]}}\,,

(170)

\bar{\Phi}_{\eta n,\eta n^{\prime}}\left(r,r^{\prime}\right)={\Theta_{n,n^{\prime}}(r-r^{\prime})\over{2\pi}}-{1\over{\pi}\,u}\int_{\{Q^{\iota}_{\tau}\}}dk\,\cos k\,{\bar{\Phi}_{c,\eta n^{\prime}}\left(\sin k/u,r^{\prime}\right)\over{n[1+({r-r\sin k/u\over n})^{2}]}}\,,

(171)

\bar{\Phi}_{\eta n,sn^{\prime}}\left(r,r^{\prime}\right)=-{1\over{\pi}\,u}\int_{\{Q^{\iota}_{\tau}\}}dk\,\cos k\,{\bar{\Phi}_{c,sn^{\prime}}\left(\sin k/u,r^{\prime}\right)\over{n[1+({r-\sin k/u\over n})^{2}]}}\,,

(172)

or in terms of both those in Eqs. (
161
)-(
163
) and in Eqs. (
167
)-(
169
),

\displaystyle{\bar{\Phi}}_{sn,c}\left(r,r^{\prime}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-{1\over\pi}\arctan\Bigl({r-r^{\prime}\over{n}}\Bigr)+{1\over{\pi}\,u}\int_{\{Q^{\iota}_{\tau}\}}dk\,\cos k\,{{\bar{\Phi}}_{c,c}\left(\sin k/u,r^{\prime}\right)\over{n[1+({r-\sin k/u\over n})^{2}]}}

(173)

\displaystyle-

\displaystyle\int_{-B/u}^{B/u}dr^{\prime\prime}{\bar{\Phi}}_{s1,c}\left(r^{\prime\prime},r^{\prime}\right){\Theta^{[1]}_{n,1}(r-r^{\prime\prime})\over{2\pi}}\,;\hskip 14.22636ptn>1\,,

\displaystyle{\bar{\Phi}}_{sn,\eta n^{\prime}}\left(r,r^{\prime}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle{1\over{\pi}\,u}\int_{\{Q^{\iota}_{\tau}\}}dk\,\cos k\,{{\bar{\Phi}}_{c,\eta n^{\prime}}\left(\sin k/u,r^{\prime}\right)\over{n[1+({r-\sin k/u\over n})^{2}]}}

(174)

\displaystyle-

\displaystyle\int_{-B/u}^{B/u}dr^{\prime\prime}{\bar{\Phi}}_{s1,\eta n^{\prime}}\left(r^{\prime\prime},r^{\prime}\right){\Theta^{[1]}_{n,1}(r-r^{\prime\prime})\over{2\pi}}\,;\hskip 14.22636ptn>1\,,

\displaystyle{\bar{\Phi}}_{sn,sn^{\prime}}\left(r,r^{\prime}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle{\Theta_{n,n^{\prime}}(r-r^{\prime})\over{2\pi}}+{1\over{\pi}\,u}\int_{\{Q^{\iota}_{\tau}\}}dk\,\cos k\,{{\bar{\Phi}}_{c,sn^{\prime}}\left(\sin k/u,r^{\prime}\right)\over{n[1+({r-\sin k/u\over n})^{2}]}}

(175)

\displaystyle-

\displaystyle\int_{-B/u}^{B/u}dr^{\prime\prime}{\bar{\Phi}}_{s1,sn^{\prime}}\left(r^{\prime\prime},r^{\prime}\right){\Theta^{[1]}_{n,1}(r-r^{\prime\prime})\over{2\pi}}\,.

In the above equations,
\Theta_{n\,n^{\prime}}(x)
is the function given in Eq. (
113
)
of Appendix
A
and
\Theta^{[1]}_{n\,n^{\prime}}(x)
is its derivative,

\displaystyle\Theta^{[1]}_{n,n^{\prime}}(x)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle{\partial\Theta_{n,n^{\prime}}(x)\over\partial x}=\delta_{n,n^{\prime}}\Bigl\{{1\over n[1+({x\over 2n})^{2}]}+\sum_{l^{\prime}=1}^{n-1}{2\over l^{\prime}[1+({x\over 2l^{\prime}})^{2}]}\Bigr\}+(1-\delta_{n,n^{\prime}})\Bigl\{{2\over|n-n^{\prime}|[1+({x\over|n-n^{\prime}|})^{2}]}

(176)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle{2\over(n+n^{\prime})[1+({x\over n+n^{\prime}})^{2}]}+\sum_{l^{\prime}=1}^{{n+n^{\prime}-|n-n^{\prime}|\over 2}-1}{4\over(|n-n^{\prime}|+2l^{\prime})[1+({x\over|n-n^{\prime}|+2l^{\prime}})^{2}]}\Bigr\}\,.

The phase-shift integral equations given here are an extension of those
provided in Ref.
Carmelo-17
, which refer to
\eta
-Bethe states generated
from ground states by small
c
and
\eta n
bands distributions deviations.

The phase shifts suitable to the set of subspaces considered in the analysis of Section

IV.2
other than the reference S
z
SLN
SN
subspace 1 to which Eqs. (
161
)-(
175
) apply
either are easily expressed in terms of those defined by these equations under suitable densities
interchange or are straightforwardly computed.

Appendix G
Useful
u\gg 1
expansions in powers of
u^{-l^{\prime}}
for
l^{\prime}=1,...,\infty

The expansion terms of
u^{-j}
order
j>2
of the
c
-band current spectrum
J_{c}^{h}(q_{j})
, Eq. (
68
),
are state dependent. To illustrate this property, in this Appendix such a current spectrum is expanded
up to
u^{-3}
order for the
m_{s}=0
and
m_{s}\rightarrow 1-m_{\eta}
reference S
z
SLN
SN
subspaces 1A
and the whole
m_{\eta}\in[0,1]
range. We consider the
\eta
-Bethe states of a general
reference S
z
SLN
SN
subspace 1A whose
\beta=c,\eta n,sn
compact distributions have limiting
momenta of the form given in Eq. (
77
). The corresponding
c
band distribution
2\pi\rho_{c}(k)

is then defined by the equation,

2\pi\rho_{c}(k)=1+\frac{\cos k}{\pi\,u}\int_{-B}^{B}d\Lambda\,{2\pi\sigma_{s1}(\Lambda)\over 1+\left({\sin k-\Lambda\over u}\right)^{2}}\hskip 5.69046pt{\rm where}\hskip 5.69046ptB=\Lambda_{0}^{\beta}(q_{Fs1})=\Lambda_{0}^{\beta}(k_{F\downarrow})\,,

(177)

and the distribution
2\pi\sigma_{s1}(\Lambda)
obeys the related equation,

2\pi\sigma_{s1}(\Lambda)={1\over\pi\,u}\int_{\{Q^{\iota}_{\tau}\}}dk\,{2\pi\rho_{c}(k)\over 1+\left({\Lambda-\sin k\over u}\right)^{2}}-\frac{1}{2\pi\,u}\int_{-B}^{B}d\Lambda^{\prime}\,{2\pi\sigma_{s1}(\Lambda^{\prime})\over 1+\left({\Lambda-\Lambda^{\prime}\over 2u})\right)^{2}}\,.

(178)

The integration
\int_{\{Q^{\iota}_{\tau}\}}
appearing here and its limiting parameters
Q^{\iota}_{\tau}
are
defined in Eqs. (
155
) and (
156
) of Appendix
F
, respectively.

As given in Eq. (
114
) of Appendix
A
, the distributions
2\pi\rho_{c}(k)
and
2\pi\sigma_{s1}(\Lambda)

are related to the inverse functions
q^{c}(k)
and
q^{s1}(\Lambda)
of the rapidity momentum functional
k^{c}(q)

and
s1
branch rapidity functional
\Lambda^{s1}(q)
, respectively, as,

2\pi\rho_{c}(k)={\partial q^{c}(k)\over\partial k}\hskip 5.69046pt{\rm and}\hskip 5.69046pt2\pi\sigma_{s1}(\Lambda)={\partial q^{s1}(\Lambda)\over\partial\Lambda}\,.

(179)

Furthermore, for the present subspace the distributions
2\pi\rho_{c}(k)
and
2\pi\sigma_{s1}(\Lambda)
obey
the sum rules,

\int_{\{Q^{\iota}_{\tau}\}}dk\,2\pi\rho_{c}(k)=2\pi(1-m_{\eta})\hskip 5.69046pt{\rm and}\hskip 5.69046pt\int_{-B}^{B}d\Lambda\,2\pi\sigma_{s1}(\Lambda)=\pi(1-m_{\eta}-m_{s})\,.

(180)

We start by considering the case of the
m_{s}=0
reference S
z
SLN
SN
subspace 1A
for densities
m_{\eta}\in[0,1]
. As for a ground state
Call-74
, in the present more general case of energy and momentum
eigenstates whose distributions are of the form, Eq. (
51
), one can use the Fourier transform
of
2\pi\sigma_{s1}(\Lambda)
to reach from the use of Eq. (
178
) the following alternative
exact relation,

2\pi\sigma_{s1}(\Lambda)={1\over 4u}\int_{\{Q^{\iota}_{\tau}\}}dk\,{2\pi\rho_{c}(k)\over\cosh\left({\pi\over 2u}(\Lambda-\sin k)\right)}\,.

(181)

The use of this
2\pi\sigma_{s1}(\Lambda)
expression in Eq. (
177
) reveals that the distribution
2\pi\rho_{c}(k)

obeys the following integral equation,

2\pi\rho_{c}(k)=1+\cos k\int_{\{Q^{\iota}_{\tau}\}}dk^{\prime}\,\Gamma(k,k^{\prime})\,2\pi\rho_{c}(k^{\prime})\,,

(182)

where the kernel reads,

\Gamma(k,k^{\prime})={1\over 4\pi}\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}d\Lambda\,\,{1\over(u^{2}+\Lambda^{2})}\,{1\over\cosh\left({\pi\over 2u}(\Lambda+\sin k-\sin k^{\prime})\right)}\,.

(183)

In order to derive a large-
u
expansion of the distribution
2\pi\rho_{c}(k)
, it is convenient to introduce
the following integral representation of the integrand factor
1/\cosh(\pi x/2u)
in Eq. (
183
)
where
x=\Lambda+\sin k-\sin k^{\prime}
,

{1\over\cosh\left({\pi x\over 2u}\right)}={1\over\pi}\int_{0}^{\infty}dy{\cos\left({xy\over 2u}\right)\over\cosh\left({y\over 2}\right)}\,.

(184)

The use of this expression in Eq. (
183
) leads to,

\Gamma(k,k^{\prime})={1\over 2\pi\,u}\int_{0}^{\infty}dy{\cos\left({(\sin k-\sin k^{\prime})y\over 2u}\right)\over 1+e^{y}}\,.

(185)

This expression is suitable for deriving the following
\Gamma(k,k^{\prime})
large-
u
expansion,

\displaystyle\Gamma(k,k^{\prime})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle{\ln 2\over 2\pi\,u}+\sum_{l^{\prime}=1}^{\infty}{(-1)^{l^{\prime}}\over 2\pi\,u}\zeta(2l^{\prime}+1)\left(1-{1\over 2^{2l^{\prime}}}\right)\left({\sin k-\sin k^{\prime}\over 2u}\right)^{2l^{\prime}}

(186)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle{\ln 2\over 2\pi\,u}+{1\over\pi}\sum_{l^{\prime}=1}^{\infty}(-1)^{l^{\prime}}{\zeta(2l^{\prime}+1)\over(2u)^{2l^{\prime}+1}}\left(1-{1\over 2^{2l^{\prime}}}\right)\sum_{l^{\prime\prime}=0}^{2l}(-1)^{l^{\prime\prime}}(\sin k^{\prime})^{l^{\prime\prime}}{2l\choose l^{\prime\prime}}(\sin k)^{2l^{\prime}-l^{\prime\prime}}\,.

From the use of this large-
u
expansion in Eq. (
182
) accounting for the first sum rule
in Eq. (
180
), the following
u^{-l^{\prime}}
expansion obeyed by the
distribution
2\pi\rho_{c}(k)
, which contains all infinite orders
l^{\prime}=1,2,...,\infty
, is
straightforwardly derived,

\displaystyle 2\pi\rho_{c}(k)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 1-{(1-m_{\eta})\ln 2\over u}\cos k

(187)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\cos k\sum_{l^{\prime}=1}^{\infty}{\zeta(2l^{\prime}+1)\over(2u)^{2l^{\prime}+1}}\left(1-{1\over 2^{2l^{\prime}}}\right)\sum_{l^{\prime\prime}=0}^{2l}(-1)^{l^{\prime}+l^{\prime\prime}}M_{l^{\prime\prime}}{2l^{\prime}\choose l^{\prime\prime}}(\sin k)^{2l^{\prime}-l^{\prime\prime}}\hskip 5.69046pt{\rm for}\hskip 5.69046ptm_{s}=0\,,

where

M_{l^{\prime\prime}}={1\over\pi}\int_{\{Q^{\iota}_{\tau}\}}dk^{\prime}\,2\pi\rho_{c}(k^{\prime})\,(\sin k^{\prime})^{l^{\prime\prime}}\,.

(188)

The function
q^{c}(k)
in Eq. (
179
), such that
2\pi\rho_{c}(k)=\partial q^{c}(k)/\partial k

and
q^{c}(0)=0
, which is the inverse function of
k^{c}(q)
, obeys a corresponding equation expanded in powers of
u^{-l^{\prime}}
,

\displaystyle q^{c}(k)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle k-{(1-m_{\eta})\ln 2\over u}\sin k

(189)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\sum_{l^{\prime}=1}^{\infty}{\zeta(2l^{\prime}+1)\over(2u)^{2l^{\prime}+1}}\left(1-{1\over 2^{2l^{\prime}}}\right)\sum_{l^{\prime\prime}=0}^{2l^{\prime}}{(-1)^{l^{\prime}+l^{\prime\prime}}M_{l^{\prime\prime}}\over(2l^{\prime}-l^{\prime\prime}+1)}{2l\choose l^{\prime\prime}}(\sin k)^{2l^{\prime}-l^{\prime\prime}+1}\,.

In contrast to ground-state particle-like symmetrical and compact
c
band distributions, Eq. (
59
),
for which the coefficients
M_{l^{\prime\prime}}
, Eq. (
188
), vanish for
l^{\prime\prime}
odd integers, in the case of the
\eta
-Bethe
states considered here the contributions from such
l^{\prime\prime}
odd integers is behind
2\pi\rho_{c}(k)
not being
a pure even function. This also implies that
q^{c}(k)
is not a pure odd function. Note though that

q^{c}(0)=0
and
q^{c}(\pm\pi)=\pm\pi
, as straightforwardly follows from analysis of Eq. (
189
).

Equations (
187
) and (
188
) can be solved order by order in
u^{-1}
.
Accounting for the first sum rule in Eq. (
180
), this gives
for instance up to
u^{-3}
order the following expansions for
2\pi\rho_{c}(k)
and
q^{c}(k)
,

\displaystyle 2\pi\rho_{c}(k)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 1+{(1-m_{\eta})\ln 2\over u}\cos k

(190)

\displaystyle-

\displaystyle{3\zeta(3)\over 32\,u^{3}}\left((1-m_{\eta})(1+2\sin^{2}k)+{\tau\over\pi}\sum_{\iota=\pm}(\iota)\left(2\cos(q_{Fc,\tau}^{\iota})\sin k-{1\over 4}\sin(2q_{Fc,\tau}^{\iota})\right)\right)\cos k\,,

and

\displaystyle q^{c}(k)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle k+{(1-m_{\eta})\ln 2\over u}\sin k

(191)

\displaystyle-

\displaystyle{3\zeta(3)\over 32\,u^{3}}\left((1-m_{\eta})\left(1+{2\over 3}\sin^{2}k\right)+{\tau\over\pi}\sum_{\iota=\pm}(\iota)\left(\cos(q_{Fc,\tau}^{\iota})\sin k-{1\over 4}\sin(2q_{Fc,\tau}^{\iota})\right)\right)\sin k\,,

respectively.

Inversion of this
q^{c}(k)
expansion gives up to
u^{-3}
order the following expression for
the rapidity momentum functional
k^{c}(q_{j})
,

\displaystyle k^{c}(q_{j})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle q_{j}-{(1-m_{\eta})\ln 2\over u}\sin q_{j}

(192)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle{((1-m_{\eta})\ln 2)^{2}\over u^{2}}\cos q_{j}\sin q_{j}-{((1-m_{\eta})\ln 2)^{3}\over u^{3}}\left(1-{3\over 2}\sin^{2}q_{j}\right)\sin q_{j}

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle{3\zeta(3)\over 32\,u^{3}}\left((1-m_{\eta})\left(1+{2\over 3}\sin^{2}q_{j}\right)+{\tau\over\pi}\sum_{\iota=\pm}(\iota)\left(\cos(q_{Fc,\tau}^{\iota})\sin q_{j}-{1\over 4}\sin(2q_{Fc,\tau}^{\iota})\right)\right)\sin q_{j}\,.

The use of the expansions, Eqs. (
190
) and (
192
), in the current spectrum
J_{c}^{h}(q_{j})

expression, Eq. (
68
), readily leads to the expansion up to
u^{-3}
order of that spectrum
given in Eq. (
72
).

In the case of the
m_{s}\rightarrow 1-m_{\eta}
reference S
z
SLN
SN
subspace 1A also considered here,
one straightforwardly finds from the use of Eqs.
(
177
)-(
180
) that for
m_{\eta}\in[0,1]
and up to second order in
(1-m_{\eta}-m_{s})\ll 1

the
c
-band distribution
2\pi\rho_{c}(k)
is for
m_{\eta}\in[0,1]
given by,

2\pi\rho_{c}(k)=1+{(1-m_{\eta}-m_{s})\over u}{\cos k\over 1+\left({\sin k\over u}\right)^{2}}+{\cal{O}}((1-m_{\eta}-m_{s})^{3})\hskip 5.69046pt{\rm for}\hskip 5.69046pt(1-m_{\eta}-m_{s})\ll 1\,.

(193)

This result actually holds true as well for any
s1
-band distribution other than that considered
in Eqs. (
178
) and (
180
).

That
2\pi\rho_{c}(k)
, Eq. (
193
), is an even function of
k
implies that
the function
q^{c}(k)
in Eq. (
179
) is an odd function of that variable. Hence it reads,

q^{c}(k)=k+(1-m_{\eta}-m_{s})\arctan\left({\sin k\over u}\right)+{\cal{O}}((1-m_{\eta}-m_{s})^{3})\hskip 5.69046pt{\rm for}\hskip 5.69046pt(1-m_{\eta}-m_{s})\ll 1\,.

(194)

Expanding
q^{c}(k)
up to
u^{-3}
order in
u^{-1}
and second order in
(1-m_{\eta}-m_{s})

and inverting that expansion gives up to
u^{-3}
order the following expression for
the rapidity momentum functional
k^{c}(q_{j})
,

\displaystyle k^{c}(q_{j})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle q_{j}-{(1-m_{\eta}-m_{s})\over u}\sin q_{j}+{(1-m_{\eta}-m_{s})^{2}\over u^{2}}\cos q_{j}\sin q_{j}

(195)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle{(1-m_{\eta}-m_{s})\over 3u^{3}}\sin^{3}q_{j}+{\cal{O}}((1-m_{\eta}-m_{s})^{3})\,.

The use of the expansions, Eqs. (
193
) and (
195
), in the current spectrum
J_{c}^{h}(q_{j})

expression, Eq. (
68
), leads to the expansion up to
u^{-3}
order of that spectrum provided in Eq.
(
73
).

\delta\langle\hat{J}_{LWS}(l_{\rm r},S_{\eta},u)\rangle=\sum_{j=1}^{L}\,\delta N_{c}(q_{j})\,\,j_{c}(q_{j})\,.

(196)

By combining the exact property that

\displaystyle|\langle\hat{J}_{LWS}^{\rm max}(l_{\rm r},S_{\eta},u)\rangle|

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 4t\,L\,m_{\eta}\,(1-m_{\eta})\hskip 5.69046pt{\rm for}\hskip 5.69046ptm_{\eta}\rightarrow 0\,,\hskip 5.69046ptm_{s}\in[0,1]\,,\hskip 5.69046pt{\rm and}\hskip 5.69046ptu\rightarrow 0

(197)

\displaystyle 2t\,L\,m_{\eta}\,(1-m_{\eta})\hskip 5.69046pt{\rm for}\hskip 5.69046ptm_{\eta}\in[0,1]\,,\hskip 5.69046ptm_{s}\rightarrow 1-m_{\eta}\,,\hskip 5.69046pt{\rm and}\hskip 5.69046ptu\rightarrow 0\,,

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2t\,L\,m_{\eta}\,(1-m_{\eta})\hskip 5.69046pt{\rm for}\hskip 5.69046ptm_{\eta}\rightarrow 1\,,\hskip 5.69046ptm_{s}\rightarrow 0\,,\hskip 5.69046pt{\rm and}\hskip 5.69046ptu\rightarrow 0\,.

The use of the expansions up to
u^{-3}
order of
J_{c}^{h}(q_{j})
, Eqs. (

expansion, Eq. (

in the cases of

reference S

SLN

u^{-3}
order
that leads to the expansions in Eqs. (

) and (

), respectively.

Moreover, the use of the specific limiting occupancy momenta
q_{Fc,\tau}^{\iota}
, Eq. (

),
that maximize the absolute values of the

SLN

\displaystyle|\langle\hat{J}_{LWS}^{\rm max}(l_{\rm r},S_{\eta},u)\rangle|

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle{2t\,L\,\sin(\pi m_{\eta})\over\pi}\left(1-{7\over 2}\left({n_{\eta s}\over u}\right)^{2}\left(1-{8\over 7}\cos(\pi m_{\eta})-{3\over 7}\sin^{2}(\pi m_{\eta})\right)\right)+{\cal{O}}(u^{-4})

(198)

\displaystyle\hskip 5.69046pt{\rm for}\hskip 5.69046ptm_{\eta}\in\left[0,{1\over 2}-\delta_{\eta s}^{u}\right]\hskip 5.69046pt{\rm and}\hskip 5.69046ptm_{s}\in[0,(1-m_{\eta})]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle{2t\,L\,\sin^{2}(\pi m_{\eta})\over\pi}+{t\,L\,n_{\eta s}\,\sin^{2}(2\pi m_{\eta})\over\pi\,u}\left(1+{3n_{\eta s}\over 2u}\cos(2\pi m_{\eta})\right)+{\cal{O}}(u^{-3})

\displaystyle\hskip 5.69046pt{\rm for}\hskip 5.69046ptm_{\eta}\in\left[{1\over 2}-\delta_{\eta s}^{u},{1\over 2}+\delta_{\eta s}^{u}\right]\hskip 5.69046pt{\rm and}\hskip 5.69046ptm_{s}\in[0,(1-m_{\eta})]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle{2t\,L\,\sin(\pi m_{\eta})\over\pi}\left(1-{7\over 2}\left({n_{\eta s}\over u}\right)^{2}\left(1+{8\over 7}\cos(\pi m_{\eta})-{3\over 7}\sin^{2}(\pi m_{\eta})\right)\right)+{\cal{O}}(u^{-4})

\displaystyle\hskip 5.69046pt{\rm for}\hskip 5.69046ptm_{\eta}\in\left[{1\over 2}+\delta_{\eta s}^{u},1\right]\hskip 5.69046pt{\rm and}\hskip 5.69046ptm_{s}\in[0,(1-m_{\eta})]\,.

Both for

,

In the case of the expansions of

\displaystyle|\langle\hat{J}_{LWS}^{\rm max(3)}(l_{\rm r},S_{\eta},u)\rangle|

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 0+{\cal{O}}(u^{-4})\hskip 5.69046pt{\rm for}\hskip 5.69046ptm_{\eta}\in\left[0,{1\over 2}-\delta_{\eta s}^{u}\right]\hskip 5.69046pt{\rm and}\hskip 5.69046ptm_{s}=0

(199)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle{2t\,L\,\sin^{2}(2\pi m_{\eta})\over\pi\,u^{3}}\{((1-m_{\eta})\ln 2)^{3}\left(1-{4\over 3}\sin^{2}(2\pi m_{\eta})\right)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle{3\zeta(3)\over 64}\left((1-m_{\eta})\left(1+{2\over 3}\sin^{2}(2\pi m_{\eta})\right)+\left(1-{1\over 2}\cos(2\pi m_{\eta})\right){\sin(2\pi m_{\eta})\over\pi}\right)\}

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle{\cal{O}}(u^{-4})\hskip 5.69046pt{\rm for}\hskip 5.69046ptm_{\eta}\in\left[{1\over 2}-\delta_{\eta s}^{u},{1\over 2}+\delta_{\eta s}^{u}\right]\hskip 5.69046pt{\rm and}\hskip 5.69046ptm_{s}=0

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 0+{\cal{O}}(u^{-4})\hskip 5.69046pt{\rm for}\hskip 5.69046ptm_{\eta}\in\left[{1\over 2}+\delta_{\eta s}^{u},1\right]\hskip 5.69046pt{\rm and}\hskip 5.69046ptm_{s}=0\,,

and

\displaystyle|\langle\hat{J}_{LWS}^{\rm max(3)}(l_{\rm r},S_{\eta},u)\rangle|

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 0+{\cal{O}}((1-m_{\eta}-m_{s})^{3})\hskip 5.69046pt{\rm for}\hskip 5.69046ptm_{\eta}\in\left[0,{1\over 2}-\delta_{\eta s}^{u}\right]\hskip 5.69046pt{\rm and}\hskip 5.69046ptm_{s}\rightarrow 1-m_{\eta}

(200)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-{t\,L\sin^{4}(2\pi m_{\eta})\over 3\pi u^{3}}\,(1-m_{\eta}-m_{s})+{\cal{O}}((1-m_{\eta}-m_{s})^{3})

\displaystyle\hskip 5.69046pt{\rm for}\hskip 5.69046ptm_{\eta}\in\left[{1\over 2}-\delta_{\eta s}^{u},{1\over 2}+\delta_{\eta s}^{u}\right]\hskip 5.69046pt{\rm and}\hskip 5.69046ptm_{s}\rightarrow 1-m_{\eta}\,,

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 0+{\cal{O}}((1-m_{\eta}-m_{s})^{3})\hskip 5.69046pt{\rm for}\hskip 5.69046ptm_{\eta}\in\left[{1\over 2}+\delta_{\eta s}^{u},1\right]\hskip 5.69046pt{\rm and}\hskip 5.69046ptm_{s}\rightarrow 1-m_{\eta}\,,

respectively.

Appendix H
Derivation of the
T\rightarrow\infty
charge stiffness upper bound
and comparison to the Mazur’s lower bound

The first goal of this appendix is to confirm that the charge stiffness upper bound
in Eq. (
106
) is larger than that given in Eq. (
105
).
At fixed density
m_{s}
, the charge currents
\langle\hat{J}_{LWS}(l_{\rm r},S_{\eta},u)\rangle=\sum_{j=1}^{L}\,N_{c}^{h}(q_{j})\,\,J_{c}^{h}(q_{j})

in Eq. (
68
) are the same for all

{L/2-S_{\eta}+S_{s}\choose 2S_{s}}
independent spin configurations with
the same spin
S_{s}=0,1,...,(L-2S_{\eta})/2
. Those generate
the spin degrees of freedom of the
\eta
-Bethe states that span a S
z
SLN
{}_{N_{1}}
subspace as defined in Section
VI

and thus contribute to the charge stiffness upper bound, Eq. (
105
). Indeed, such currents only
depend on the density
m_{s}
common to all such spin configurations through the dependence
on that density of the charge current spectrum
J_{c}^{h}(q_{j})=-J_{c}(q_{j})
in Eq. (
68
).

At fixed density
m_{\eta}
, the charge currents in Eq. (
68
) directly depend on the occupancy
configurations of the
N_{c}=(L-2S_{\eta})=L\,(1-m_{\eta})
charge
c
pseudoparticles over the
available
j=1,...,L

c
-band discrete momentum values
q_{j}
of which
N_{c}^{h}=2S_{\eta}=L\,m_{\eta}

are unoccupied. Such currents also depend on the density
m_{\eta}
common to all such
charge configurations through the dependence on that density of the charge current spectrum

J_{c}^{h}(q_{j})=-J_{c}(q_{j})
in Eq. (
68
). The set of
j=1,...,L

c
-band discrete momentum values

\{q_{j}\}
are exactly the same for all
\eta
-Bethe states that span the S
z
SLN
{}_{N_{1}}
subspace
under consideration.

Hence concerning the spin occupancy configurations and
c
-band occupancy configurations
that generate such states, only the latter determine the form of the charge currents in Eq. (
68
).
The only effect of the spin degrees of freedom onto the charge currents is the
m_{s}

dependence of the current spectrum
J_{c}^{h}(q_{j})=-J_{c}(q_{j})
in Eq. (
68
).

The S
z
SLN
{}_{N_{1}}
subspace considered here can be divided into references S
z
SLN
SN
subspaces 1A,
each with a fixed spin belonging to the set
S_{s}=0,1,...,(L-2S_{\eta})/2
. One can then choose the fixed-
S_{s}

reference S
z
SLN
SN
subspace 1A whose corresponding charge stiffness upper bound is larger
than that in Eq. (
105
), which is defined within the whole S
z
SLN
{}_{N_{1}}
subspace.
It follows from the above properties that such a fixed-
S_{s}
subspace
is that for which the average
|{\bar{J}}_{c}|={1\over L}\sum_{j=1}^{L}|J_{c}(q_{j})|
,
Eq. (
132
) of Appendix
C
for
\beta=c
,
of the absolute value
|J_{c}(q_{j})|
of the charge current spectrum

J_{c}^{h}(q_{j})=-J_{c}(q_{j})
in Eq. (
68
) is largest.
Such an average is independent of the
q_{j}
occupancies and runs over all

j=1,...,L
such momentum values. Within the TL, one
replaces the discrete momentum values
q_{j}
such that
q_{j+1}-q_{j}=2\pi/L

by a continuum momentum variable
q
. By replacing the sum
\sum_{j=1}^{L}

by an integral it is found that up to
u^{-2}
order such an average value reads,

|{\bar{J}}_{c}|={4t\over\pi}\left(1+3\left({(1-m_{\eta}-m_{s})\,g_{s}\over u}\right)^{2}\right)\,,

(201)

where
g_{s}=g_{s}(m_{s})
is the function in Eq. (
71
).

Suitable analysis of this expression reveals that
|{\bar{J}}_{c}(q_{j})|
is largest for the
S_{s}=0

reference S
z
SLN
SN
subspace 1A. This follows from the inequality,

\ln 2>{(1-m_{\eta}-m_{s})\over(1-m_{\eta})}\,g_{s}\hskip 9.95863pt{\rm for}\hskip 9.95863ptm_{s}\in[0,1-m_{\eta}]\hskip 9.95863pt{\rm where}\hskip 9.95863ptg_{s}\in[\ln 2,1]\,.

(202)

One finds that the derivative
\partial g_{s}(m_{s})/\partial m_{s}
is such that

\partial g_{s}(m_{s})/\partial m_{s}|_{m_{s}=0}=0
at
m_{s}=0
and

\partial g_{s}(m_{s})/\partial m_{s}|_{m_{s}}>0
for
m_{s}\in]0,(1-m_{\eta})]
.
The use of such derivative behaviors confirms the validity of the inequality, Eq. (
202
).

Although for simplicity here it was confirmed that the charge stiffness upper bound
in Eq. (
106
) is larger than that given in Eq. (
105
)
for the approximate
u>3/2
range for which the
u^{-2}
order expansions of these upper bounds
apply, the validity of the result can be shown to apply to the whole
u>0
range.
That here its validity was confirmed up to
u^{-2}
order stems from the charge stiffness
upper bound, Eq. (
106
), being computed in the following to that order.

The second goal of this Appendix is to determine an exact expression for that charge stiffness
upper bound up to
u^{-2}
order and in the TL for the hole concentration range

m_{\eta}^{z}\in[0,1/2]
. We start by decomposing the current
J_{c}(q_{j})=-J_{c}^{h}(q_{j})
, Eq. (
107
),
into a polynomial in
N_{c}=L-2S_{\eta}
,

J_{c}(q_{j})=\sum_{\mu=0}^{2}\alpha_{\mu}(q_{j})N_{c}^{\mu}\,,

(203)

where

\alpha_{0}(q_{j})=-2t\sin q_{j},\quad\alpha_{1}(q_{j})=2t\,\frac{\ln 2}{L\,u}\,\sin 2q_{j},\quad\alpha_{2}(q_{j})=-6t\,\frac{(\ln 2)^{2}}{L^{2}\,u^{2}}\,\left(1-\frac{3}{2}\sin^{2}q_{j}\right)\sin q_{j}\,.

(204)

We use a simplified notation within which
N_{c}(q_{j})=:\nu_{j}\in\{0,1\}
are the binary occupation numbers and
N_{c}=\sum_{j}\nu_{j}=L-2S_{\eta}
.
The main technical step is is the evaluation of the many-body sum (which is just the sum over
\sum_{l^{*}}
in
Eq. (
106
) without the constant
1/(2S_{\eta})^{2}
prefactor),

I_{N_{c}}=\sum_{\{\nu_{j}\}\in\{0,1\}^{L}}\left(\sum_{j=1}^{L}\nu_{j}J_{j}\right)^{2}\delta_{N_{c},\sum_{j}\nu_{j}}=\sum_{\mu,\mu^{\prime}=0}^{2}\phi_{\mu,\mu^{\prime}}(N_{c})\,N^{\mu+\mu^{\prime}}\,,

(205)

where
J_{j}=J_{c}(q_{j})
and,

\phi_{\mu,\mu^{\prime}}(N_{c})=\sum_{\{\nu_{j}\}\in\{0,1\}^{L}}\delta_{N_{c},\sum_{j}\nu_{j}}\sum_{j,k=1}^{L}\alpha_{\mu}(q_{j})\,\alpha_{\mu^{\prime}}(q_{k})\,\nu_{j}\,\nu_{k}\,.

(206)

This object is in turn evaluated via writing its discrete Laplace transform,

\displaystyle\tilde{\phi}_{\mu,\mu^{\prime}}(\lambda)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{N_{c}=0}^{L}e^{\lambda N_{c}}\phi_{\mu,\mu^{\prime}}(N_{c})

(207)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{\{\nu_{j}\}}\left(\prod_{n=1}^{L}e^{\lambda\nu_{n}}\right)\sum_{j,k}\alpha_{\mu}(q_{j})\,\alpha_{\mu^{\prime}}(q_{k})\,\nu_{j}\,\nu_{k}

(208)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(1+e^{\lambda})^{L-2}e^{2\lambda}\sum_{j,k}^{j\neq k}\alpha_{\mu}(q_{j})\,\alpha_{\mu^{\prime}}(q_{k})+(1+e^{\lambda})^{L-1}e^{\lambda}\sum_{j}\alpha_{\mu}(q_{j})\,\alpha_{\mu^{\prime}}(q_{j})

(209)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(1+e^{\lambda})^{L-2}e^{\lambda}A_{\mu,\mu^{\prime}}\,,

(210)

where we accounted for that
\sum_{j}\alpha_{\mu}(q_{j})=0
for all
\mu
and defined the sums, or integrals,

A_{\mu,\nu}:=\sum_{j}\alpha_{\mu}(q_{j})\,\alpha_{\mu^{\prime}}(q_{j})\simeq\frac{L}{2\pi}\int_{-\pi}^{\pi}dq\,\alpha_{\mu}(q)\,\alpha_{\mu^{\prime}}(q)\,,

(211)

which can be straightforwardly computed. From there we read,

\phi_{\mu,\mu^{\prime}}(N_{c})={L-2\choose N_{c}-1}A_{\mu,\mu^{\prime}}\hskip 5.69046pt{\rm and}\hskip 5.69046pt\phi_{\mu,\mu^{\prime}}(0)=\phi_{\mu,\mu^{\prime}}(L-1)=0\,.

(212)

Plugging all that to the charge stiffness upper bound, Eq. (
106
), we find,

D^{\diamond\diamond}(T)={(2S_{\eta}^{z})^{2}\over 2LT}\,\,{\sum_{S_{\eta}=|S_{\eta}^{z}|}^{L/2}\sum_{\mu,\mu^{\prime}=0}^{2}\frac{1}{(2S_{\eta})^{2}}{L-2\choose L-2S_{\eta}-1}A_{\mu,\mu^{\prime}}(L-2S_{\eta})^{\mu+\mu^{\prime}}\over\sum_{S_{\eta}=|S_{\eta}^{z}|}^{L/2}{L\choose 2S_{\eta}}}\,.

(213)

Next we perform an asymptotic analysis of this expression, accounting for that
L\rightarrow\infty
within the present TL. First we note that,

{L-2\choose L-2S_{\eta}-1}\simeq\frac{2S_{\eta}(L-2S_{\eta})}{L^{2}}{L\choose 2S_{\eta}}\,.

(214)

Then we realize that both sums over the binomial symbols in the numerator and the denominator of the expression, Eq. (
213
),
become sharply peaked around
S_{\eta}=S^{*}=L/4
, under the condition that,

|S^{z}_{\eta}|<\frac{L}{4}\,,

(215)

and thus
m_{\eta}^{z}\in[0,1/2]
.

Finally, the charge stiffness upper bound, Eq. (
106
), then reads within the TL and
for
m_{\eta}^{z}\in[0,1/2]
,

D^{\diamond\diamond}(T)={(2S_{\eta}^{z})^{2}\over 2LT}\sum_{\mu,\mu^{\prime}=0}^{2}A_{\mu,\mu^{\prime}}L^{-2}(L/2)^{\mu+\mu^{\prime}}=\left(\frac{2S^{z}_{\eta}}{L}\right)^{2}\frac{\pi^{2}t^{2}}{T}\left(1+\left(\frac{\ln 2}{2u}\right)^{2}+{\cal O}(u^{-4})\right)\,,

(216)

which can indeed be written as given in Eq. (
108
).

The charge stiffness Mazur’s lower bound has been derived for
T\rightarrow\infty
in Ref.
ZNP-97
.
In the zero-spin case considered in the upper-bound studies of this paper one finds that
the charge stiffness Mazur’s lower bound
D^{\rm Mz}(T)
is such that,

D(t)\geq D^{\rm Mz}(T)=\frac{c_{\rm Mz}\,t^{2}}{2T}(m^{z}_{\eta})^{2}\hskip 5.69046pt{\rm where}\hskip 5.69046ptc_{\rm Mz}={2\,(1-(m^{z}_{\eta})^{2})\over(1+(m^{z}_{\eta})^{2})\left(1+\left({1\over u}\right)^{2}{1\over 8(1+(m^{z}_{\eta})^{2})}\right)}\,.

(217)

On the one hand, for
(1-m^{z}_{\eta})\ll 1
and up to
{\cal{O}}(u^{-2})
order the upper bound, Eq. (
110
),
equals the charge stiffness. Hence one finds to such an order that in that limit for which

c_{\rm Mz}\approx 2(1-m^{z}_{\eta})(1-(1/4u)^{2})
the use of the Mazur’s lower bound leads to the inequality,

D(T)=\frac{2t^{2}}{2T}(1-m^{z}_{\eta})\geq\frac{2t^{2}}{2T}(1-m^{z}_{\eta})\left(1-\left({1\over 4u}\right)^{2}\right)\hskip 5.69046pt{\rm for}\hskip 5.69046pt(1-m^{z}_{\eta})\ll 1\,.

(218)

For
(1-m^{z}_{\eta})\ll 1
the Mazur’s lower bound thus equals the charge stiffness only in the
u\rightarrow\infty
limit.

On the other hand, for
m^{z}_{\eta}\ll 1
one finds up to
{\cal{O}}(u^{-2})
order that

c_{\rm Mz}\approx 2(1-((1/\sqrt{2})/2u)^{2})
and the charge stiffness is of the form
D(t)=\frac{c_{u}\,t^{2}}{2T}(m^{z}_{\eta})^{2}

where the coefficient
c_{u}
obeys the double inequality given in Eq. (
109
).
